Appendix H
Proofs

We omit the completely standard proof that basic typing

\vdash_{{\tt s}}e:s
is sound, assuming that all terms and qualifiers
in our typing rules and theorems are type-safe. Before presenting the
proof of the fundamental theorem and type soundness, we introduce
several useful lemmas.

H.1.
Lemmas

H.1.1.
Denotations

Lemma H.1.

[Denotation of singleton modality] For all symbolic event
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{x_{i}}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}
and values
\overline{v_{i}}
,

\displaystyle\phi[\overline{{x_{i}}\mapsto{v_{i}}}]\implies[\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}({\overline{v_{i}}})]\in\llbracket{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{x_{i}}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}}\rrbracket

Lemma H.2.

[Denotation of concatenation] For all automata
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
and trace
\alpha
,

\displaystyle\alpha\in\llbracket{A_{1}{\cdot}A_{2}}\rrbracket\iff(\exists\alpha_{1}\;\alpha_{2}.\alpha=\alpha_{1}{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\begin{picture}\roundcap\put(0.1,0.5){\line{1}{0}{0.8}}\put(0.35,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\put(0.65,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\end{picture}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\begin{picture}\roundcap\put(0.1,0.5){\line{1}{0}{0.8}}\put(0.35,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\put(0.65,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\end{picture}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\begin{picture}\roundcap\put(0.1,0.5){\line{1}{0}{0.8}}\put(0.35,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\put(0.65,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\end{picture}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\begin{picture}\roundcap\put(0.1,0.5){\line{1}{0}{0.8}}\put(0.35,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\put(0.65,0.1){\line{0}{1}{0.8}}\end{picture}}}}}}\alpha_{2}\land\alpha_{1}\in\llbracket{A_{1}}\rrbracket\land\alpha_{2}\in\llbracket{A_{2}}\rrbracket)

Lemma H.3.

[Denotation of pure computation] For all term
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
and
Pret

\tau
,

\displaystyle(\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{e}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{e^{\prime}}){\implies}e^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\tau}\rrbracket{\implies}e\in\llbracket{\tau}\rrbracket

H.1.2.
Subtyping

Lemma H.4.

[Pure Subtyping Soundness] For Given type context
\Gamma
and well-formed pure refinement type
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
:

\Gamma\vdash t_{1}<:t_{2}\implies\forall\sigma_{2}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\exists\sigma_{1}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\llbracket{\sigma_{2}(t_{2})}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket{\sigma_{1}(t_{1})}\rrbracket

Lemma H.5.

[Inclusion] For Given type context
\Gamma
and well-formed regex
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
:

\Gamma\vdash H_{1}\subseteq H_{2}\implies\forall\sigma_{2}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\exists\sigma_{1}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\llbracket{\sigma_{2}(H_{2})}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket{\sigma_{1}(H_{1})}\rrbracket

Lemma H.6.

[Subtyping Soundness] For Given type context
\Gamma
and well-formed
Pret

\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
:

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}<:\tau_{2}\implies\forall\sigma_{2}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\exists\sigma_{1}\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\llbracket{\sigma_{2}(\tau_{2})}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket{\sigma_{1}(\tau_{1})}\rrbracket

H.1.3.
Substitution

Lemma H.7 (Substitution Lemma).

For Given type context
\Gamma
, variable
x
, well-formed pure refinement type
t
,
Pret

\tau
and term
e
:

\Gamma,x{:}t\vdash e:\tau{\implies}\forall v.\Gamma\vdash v:t{\implies}\Gamma,x{:}t\vdash e[x\mapsto v]:\tau[x\mapsto v]

H.1.4.
Type Contexts

In the
Sec. 3
, we defined the well-formedness of effectful operator typing context, we also define the well-formedness of pure operator typing context.

Definition H.8 (Well-formed pure operator typing context).

The operator typing context
\Delta
is well-formed
iff the semantics of every pure operator
\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}
is
consistent with the type
\overline{x{:}b_{\mathit{x}}}{\,\dashrightarrow\,}\overline{y{:}t_{\mathit{y}}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}t
provided by
\Delta
.

\displaystyle\overline{\forall x{:}b_{\mathit{x}}}.\;\overline{\forall y\in\llbracket{t_{\mathit{y}}}\rrbracket}.\;\forall c\in\llbracket{t}\rrbracket.c^{\prime}\in\llbracket{t_{\mathit{x}}[\overline{{y}\mapsto{c}}]}\rrbracket{\implies}\mathit{op}(c)\Downarrow c^{\prime}

The typing relation is defined mutually recursively between values, terms and operators, that is:

Theorem H.9.

[Pure Fundamental Theorem] For Given type context
\Gamma
, term
e
and value
v
:

\Gamma\vdash v:t\implies\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v)\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket
and

\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\implies\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(e)\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau)}\rrbracket
and

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:t\implies\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket
and

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{op}:\overline{y{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}t\implies\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\mathit{op}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction over our type judgment
\Gamma\vdash e:\tau
and
\Gamma\vdash v:t
, which has cases proved as following:

Case

:

TConst

\lfloor{\Gamma}\rfloor\vdash_{{\tt s}}c:b

\Gamma\vdash c:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\nu=c}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}

Since the constant
c
is closed, we have
\sigma(c)=c
for any substitution
\sigma
. By the semantics of evaluation,
{\alpha}\vDash{c}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{c}
holds trivially. Therefore,
c\in\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\nu=c}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}}\rrbracket
directly by the definition of type denotation. This concludes the case.

Case

:

TVar

\lfloor{\Gamma}\rfloor\vdash_{{\tt s}}x:b

\Gamma\vdash x:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\nu=x}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}

Since the variable
x
is will typed under the context
\Gamma
in the basic type system, thus there exists a pure refinement type
t
such that
(x,t)\in\Gamma
. The denotation of
\Gamma
will always contains a substitution
\sigma
such that
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}[x\mapsto v]
where
v\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket
. Therefore,
\sigma(x)=v
, and
\sigma(x)\in\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\nu=v}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}}\rrbracket
. This concludes the case.

Case

:

TFun

\Gamma,x{:}t_{x}\vdash e:\tau

\Gamma\vdash\lambda{x}.{e}:x{:}t_{x}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}\tau

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma,x{:}t_{x}\vdash e:\tau

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma,x{:}t_{x}}\rrbracket.\sigma(e)\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}[x\mapsto v]\hskip 8.50012pt\text{where}\hskip 8.50012pt\sigma^{\prime}\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\land v\in\llbracket{\sigma^{\prime}(t_{x})}\rrbracket

(denotation of type context and
2
)

(4)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}(e[x\mapsto v])\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau)}\rrbracket

(by
2
and
3
and Lemma
H.7
)

(5)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{\lambda{x}.{e}\;v}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{e[x\mapsto v]}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

(6)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}(\lambda{x}.{e}\;v)\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau)}\rrbracket

(by
4
and
5
and Lemma
H.3
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TFixBase

\Gamma\vdash{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.0313,0.0039,0.457}{fix}}}}}{f}.\lambda{x}.{e}:\overline{x{:}s}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\bot}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\emptyset}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

Any function can have a empty return type for free, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TFixInd

\Gamma\vdash{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.0313,0.0039,0.457}{fix}}}}}{f}.\lambda{x}.{e}:t

\Gamma,f{:}t\vdash\lambda{x}.{e}:t^{\prime}

\Gamma\vdash{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.0313,0.0039,0.457}{fix}}}}}{f}.\lambda{x}.{e}:t^{\prime}

We inline the recursion function
f
within its body, then this case is reduced to the case of function typing.

Case

:

TRet

\Gamma\vdash v:t

\Gamma\vdash v:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\epsilon}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash v:t

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v)\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{v}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{v}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TChoice

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}:\tau_{1}

\Gamma\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{2}

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}:\tau_{1}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,\tau_{2}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash e_{1}:\tau_{1}

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(e_{1})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau_{1})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash e_{2}:\tau_{2}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(e_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau_{2})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(5)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{e_{1}\oplus e_{2}}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{e_{1}}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

(6)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{e_{1}\oplus e_{2}}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{e_{2}}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

(7)

\displaystyle\sigma(e_{1}\oplus e_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau_{1})}\rrbracket

(by
2
and
5
and Lemma
H.3
)

(8)

\displaystyle\sigma(e_{1}\oplus e_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau_{2})}\rrbracket

(by
4
and
6
and Lemma
H.3
)

(9)

\displaystyle\sigma(e_{1}\oplus e_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau_{1}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,\tau_{2})}\rrbracket

(by definition of denotation)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TPureSub

\Gamma\vdash v:t

\Gamma\vdash t<:t^{\prime}

\Gamma\vdash v:t^{\prime}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash v:t

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v)\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash t<:t^{\prime}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\sigma(v)\in\llbracket{\sigma(t^{\prime})}\rrbracket

(by
2
and
3
and Lemma
H.4
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TSub

\Gamma\vdash e:\tau

\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\tau^{\prime}

\Gamma\vdash e:\tau^{\prime}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash e:\tau

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(e)\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\tau<:\tau^{\prime}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\sigma(e)\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau^{\prime})}\rrbracket

(by
2
and
3
and Lemma
H.6
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TOpHis

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\Gamma,\overline{x{:}t_{x}}\vdash H^{\prime}\subseteq H

\Gamma,\overline{x{:}t_{x}}\not\vdash H^{\prime}\subseteq\emptyset

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\Gamma,\overline{x{:}t_{x}}\vdash H^{\prime}\subseteq H

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(H^{\prime})\subseteq\sigma(H)

(by
3
and Lemma
H.5
)

(5)

\displaystyle\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(by
2
and
5
and definition
3.3
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TPureOp

\Delta(\mathit{op})=t

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{op}:t

Can be derived directly from the definition of well-formedness of pure operator typing context (Definition
H.8
).

Case

:

TOpCtx

\Delta(\boldsymbol{{\tt op}})=t

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:t

Can be derived directly from the definition of well-formedness of operator typing context (Definition
3.3
).

Case

:

TEffOp

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x_{i}{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{x_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\forall i.\;\Gamma\vdash v_{i}:t_{i}

\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}{\;\overline{v_{i}}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{v_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x_{i}{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{x_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\overline{x_{i}{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{x_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\forall i.\;\Gamma\vdash v_{i}:t_{i}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\forall i.\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v_{i})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t_{i})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(5)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{(\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\;\overline{v_{i}}}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{v_{i}}}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

(6)

\displaystyle\sigma((\lambda\overline{x}.\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{x})\;\overline{v_{i}})\in\llbracket{\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{v_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(by
5
and denotation definition)

(7)

\displaystyle\sigma(\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;\overline{v_{i}})\in\llbracket{\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}\;{\overline{v_{i}}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(by
5
and
6
and Lemma
H.3
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TOpApp

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{op}:\overline{y{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}t

\forall i.\Gamma\vdash v_{i}:t_{i}

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{op}\ \overline{v}:t

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\mathit{op}:\overline{y{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}t

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(\lambda\overline{x}.\mathit{op}\;\overline{x})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\overline{y{:}t_{i}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}t)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\forall i.\;\Gamma\vdash v_{i}:t_{i}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\forall i.\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v_{i})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t_{i})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(5)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha.{\alpha}\vDash{(\lambda\overline{y}.\mathit{op}\;\overline{y})\;\overline{v_{i}}}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{\mathit{op}\;\overline{v_{i}}}

(by the semantics of evaluation)

(6)

\displaystyle\sigma((\lambda\overline{y}.\mathit{op}\;\overline{y})\;\overline{v_{i}})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket

(by
5
and denotation definition)

(7)

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathit{op}\;\overline{v_{i}})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t)}\rrbracket

(by
5
and
6
and Lemma
H.3
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

Case

:

TAppEff

\Gamma v_{1}:x{:}t_{2}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}\tau

\Gamma\vdash v_{2}:t_{2}

\Gamma\vdash v_{1}v_{2}:\tau[x\mapsto v_{2}]

According to the assumption of this case and the hypothesis, we know:

(1)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash v_{1}:x{:}t_{2}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}\tau

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v_{1})\in\llbracket{\sigma(x{:}t_{2}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}\tau)}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(3)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash v_{2}:t_{2}

(assumption)

(4)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\sigma(v_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(t_{2})}\rrbracket

(induction hypothesis)

(5)

\displaystyle\sigma(v_{1}\;v_{2})\in\llbracket{\sigma(\tau[x\mapsto v_{2}])}\rrbracket

(by definition of denotation land Lemma
H.7
)

Then, the conclusion is consistent with the denotation definition, thus the case is proved.

∎

H.2.
Type Soundness

The type soundness can be proved by fundamental theorem directly.

Corollary H.10 (Type Soundness).

A generator
e
that satisfies

\emptyset\vdash e:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\epsilon}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\tt unit}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}
must produce all
traces accepted by
F
, i.e.,

\forall\alpha\in\llbracket{F}\rrbracket.{[]}\vDash{e}{\,\xhookrightarrow{\lceil{\alpha}\rceil}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{()}
.

Proof.

According to the fundamental theorem, we have

(1)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash e:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\epsilon}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\tt unit}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

(assumption)

(2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in\llbracket{\emptyset}\rrbracket.\sigma(e)\in\llbracket{\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\epsilon}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\tt unit}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{})}\rrbracket

(by Theorem
H.9
and
1
)

(3)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha_{f}\in\llbracket{F}\rrbracket.\exists\alpha_{h}\in\llbracket{\epsilon}\rrbracket.{\alpha_{h}}\vDash{e}{\,\xhookrightarrow{\lceil{\alpha_{f}}\rceil}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{()}

(by
2
and denotation definition)

(4)

\displaystyle\alpha_{f}\in\llbracket{\epsilon}\rrbracket\iff\alpha_{f}=[\;]

(by denotation definition)

(5)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha_{f}\in\llbracket{F}\rrbracket.{\epsilon}\vDash{e}{\,\xhookrightarrow{\lceil{\alpha_{f}}\rceil}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{()}

(by
3
and
4
and denotation definition)

The the soundness is proved.
∎

H.3.
Synthesis is Sound

As discussed in
Sec. 4
, our synthesis algorithm first
refines the input property to a set of realizable abstract
traces and then uses the
\mathbf{TermDerive}
function to translate these
traces into a controller program. We introduce a concept of realizability of abstract traces which stand as a bridge between Algorithm
2
and Algorithm
4
.

Definition H.11 (Symbolic event consistent with trace).

A symbolic event
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}
in abstract trace
\pi
(i.e.,

\pi=\pi_{h}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}{\cdot}\pi{\cdot}{\cdot}\pi_{f}
, where
\pi
is a sequence of ghost events) is

consistent
with the trace,
denoted as
\Gamma\vdash_{R}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}\in\pi
, iff

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}:\overline{x{:}t_{x}}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\pi_{h}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,x:t\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}{\cdot}\pi}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

Definition H.12 (Realizability).

An abstract trace
\pi
is realizable under type context
\Gamma
iff all concrete events in
\pi
are consistent with
\pi
under context
\Gamma
, denoted as
\Gamma\vdash_{R}\pi
.

We now prove that the refined event in Algorithm
2
is consistent with the trace.

Lemma H.13 (Refined event is consistent with trace).

The refined event in Algorithm
2
is consistent with the trace.

Proof.

Assume Algorithm
2
refines the event
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}
in the trace
\pi_{h}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}{\cdot}\pi{\cdot}{\cdot}\pi_{f}
. We then have
\pi_{h}\subseteq H
and
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}{\cdot}\pi\subseteq F
(line 4 and line 5 in Algorithm
2
), where
H
and
F
are the history and future regex of the
u
Hat
of
\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}
. According to the rule
TOpHis
, which allows for a smaller history regex, and the rule
TSub
, which allows for a smaller future regex, we have that
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt op}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}
is consistent with the trace.
∎

Next, we show that all results of Algorithm
2
are realizable traces.

Lemma H.14 (Refined traces are realizable).

Assume
(\Gamma,\pi)
is a result of Algorithm
2
. Then
\pi
is realizable under
\Gamma
, denoted as
\Gamma\vdash_{R}\pi
.

Proof.

By contradiction. If
\pi
is not realizable under
\Gamma
, then there exists a concrete event in
\pi
that is not consistent with
\Gamma
. However, according to Lemma
H.13
, the refined event is consistent with the trace, which leads to a contradiction.
∎

Now we prove the derived program is type-safe if the refined trace is realizable.

Lemma H.15 (Result of
\mathbf{TraceDerive}
is type-safe).

The program derived by
\mathbf{TraceDerive}
is type-safe if the input trace
\pi
is realizable under the input type context
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Assume the synthesized program is
e
.
Since
\mathbf{TraceDerive}
drops ghost events, we have
\forall\alpha.\,{\alpha}\vDash{e}{\,\xhookrightarrow{[]}\,\hskip-5.0pt^{*}\,}{()}
. Therefore, there exists a trace
\alpha^{\prime}
that fills the ghost events back into
\alpha
and is also consistent with the abstract trace
\pi
:

\displaystyle\exists\alpha^{\prime}.\,\lceil{\alpha^{\prime}}\rceil=\alpha\land\exists\sigma\in\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\,\alpha^{\prime}\in\sigma(\pi)

Then, we know
e\in\llbracket{\sigma{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\epsilon}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\tt unit}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\pi}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}}}\rrbracket
. Thus, according to the fundamental theorem, the derived program is type-safe.
∎

Theorem H.16 (Derived program of
\mathbf{TermDerive}
is type-safe).

If the recursion template is type-safe, then the program derived by (
\mathbf{TermDerive}
) is type-safe if all input traces are realizable under their corresponding input type contexts.

Proof.

Since every program derived by
\mathbf{TraceDerive}
is type-safe, and the recursion template guarantees that the template after filling the holes is also type-safe, each program derived by
\mathbf{TermDerive}
is type-safe.
According to the rule
TChoice
, the union (
\oplus
) of all derived programs is also type-safe.
∎

Theorem H.17 (Synthesis is Sound).

Every generator synthesized by
Algorithm 1
is type-safe
with respect to our declarative typing system.

Proof.

According to Lemma
H.14
and Lemma
H.15
, all refined traces are realizable and all derived programs are type-safe. Then, by Theorem
H.16
, the synthesized program is type-safe.
∎

Appendix I
Benchmark Specifications Explanation

This appendix lists the global safety property and the
u
Hat
s for every benchmark used in our evaluation. Each of them consistent of a short description, the global property,
u
Hat
, and the weakened
u
Hat
.

Weakening strategy.

We tie this appendix to
Sec. 2
and to the weakening study in our evaluation: we fix the SUT and the global property, and relax
u
Hat
s to measure how “lower-quality” specs affect
Clouseau
. We use two weakening strategies:

•

weakening qualifiers directly: For a refinement type
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\phi}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}
or a symbolic event
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt ev}}~|~{\phi}}{\rangle}
, we replace the qualifier
\phi
with a weaker
\phi^{\prime}
such that
\phi^{\prime}\implies\phi
. Concretely, this can drop conjuncts or weaken predicates to
\top
.

•

merge intersected types: In
u
Hat
, we use intersection types to represent different situations an operation may face. For example, an initialized system (i.e.,
H_{1}={\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt init}}\;{{\cdot}}}{\rangle}{\bullet^{*}}
) and an uninitialized system (i.e.,
H_{2}=(\bullet\setminus{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt init}}\;{)}}{\rangle}^{*}
) yield two intersected
u
Hat
s with different history automata:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\phi_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\phi_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

One weakening step merges them into a single case:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{H_{1}\lor H_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}{\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{b}\,|\,{\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{F_{1}\lor F_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

This approach is sound because it additionally allows “when
H_{1}
holds,
F_{2}
happens” and “when
H_{2}
holds,
F_{1}
happens”, compared to the original intersected
u
Hat
s.

The weakened
u
Hat
s are presented in the following sections according to these two patterns; thus, we omit most of the details of the weakening process.

Representation conventions.

For synchronous benchmarks we often split one logical operation
\boldsymbol{{\tt ev}}(\mathit{args},\mathit{return})
into
\boldsymbol{{\tt evReq}}(\mathit{args})
and
\boldsymbol{{\tt evRsp}}(\mathit{return})
, matching request/response APIs. Global properties are given in the form that the test generator is instructed to falsify (negated safety). Futures in each clause relate events across the trace and carry search bias; see
Stack
for a concrete instance.

Stack

Consider an effectful stack implemented with a fixed-size array. An incorrect implementation that fails to grow the array when the stack is full may drop
\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}
operations. To expose this bug, we ask whether traces of the following form raise an exception:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}({0});\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}({4});\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}({7});\boldsymbol{{\tt pop}}({7});\boldsymbol{{\tt pop}}({4});{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\tt pop}}({\_})\quad\text{Exception!}}

The implementation uses an underlying array of length
2
and mistakenly raises an “stack is empty” exception even though element
0
should still be on the stack. In this benchmark we split
\boldsymbol{{\tt pop}}
into
\boldsymbol{{\tt popReq}}
and
\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}
;
\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}
has no return value, so we only model
\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}
and omit
\boldsymbol{{\tt pushRsp}}
.
The global property characterizes popping “away” a pushed value
y
without observing
{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=y}}{\rangle}
, and then observing an empty stack—modeling loss of the last pushed element.

\displaystyle{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=y}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=y}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~|~{\mathit{isEmpty}}}{\rangle}

The
u
Hat
comprises the following clauses:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle y{:}{\tt int}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=y}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt popReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt initStackReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initStackReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt popReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt popReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}~:~{}

\displaystyle x{:}{\tt int}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{{\tt int}}\,|\,{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~:~{}

\displaystyle z{:}{\tt bool}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{{\tt bool}}\,|\,{\mathit{isEmpty}=z}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~|~{\mathit{isEmpty}=z}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

The future automaton in the
\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}

u
Hat
describes not only
\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}
itself, but also obligates a later
\boldsymbol{{\tt popReq}}
. That encodes a pairing constraint between
\boldsymbol{{\tt push}}
and
\boldsymbol{{\tt pop}}
, and acts as a search bias for this benchmark. Similarly, the future of
\boldsymbol{{\tt popRsp}}
forbids any later
\boldsymbol{{\tt pushReq}}
, so along such traces the stack depth changes monotonically. The weakened
u
Hat
drops these biases, yielding specifications that give the synthesizer less guidance.

Set

This benchmark shares a similar setting with
Stack
, where we have an effectful set implemented with a fixed-size array. An incorrect implementation that fails to grow the array when the set is full may drop
\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}
operations. In this benchmark, we use a ghost event to explicitly track the size of the set.
Then, the error is that, after initialization and insertion of
y
, a
mismatched
delete request (
\neg(\mathit{elem}=y)
) should not appear in a realistic violation scenario tied to size discipline; the global property targets a bad delete-generation path right after insert. Precisely, the global property is given by the following:

\displaystyle{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initSet}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=y}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\neg(\mathit{elem}=y)}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}

The
u
Hat
are shown below:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt initSet}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initSet}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}size}}}~|~{\mathit{sz}=0}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~:~{}

\displaystyle x{:}{\tt int}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~|~{\mathit{elem}\geq x}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}size}}}~|~{\mathit{sz}=i}}{\rangle}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle x{:}{\tt int}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\cdot}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}size}}}~|~{\mathit{sz}=i+1}}{\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delRsp}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}size}}}~|~{\mathit{sz}=i}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt delRsp}}~:~{}

\displaystyle r{:}{\tt bool}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{{\tt bool}}\,|\,{\mathit{res}=r}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}size}}}~:~{}

\displaystyle i{:}{\tt int}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{{\tt int}}\,|\,{\mathit{sz}=i}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~:~{}

\displaystyle b{:}{\tt bool}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{{\tt bool}}\,|\,{\mathit{isEmpty}=b}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt isEmpRsp}}~|~{\mathit{isEmpty}=b}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

The weakened
u
Hat
drops much of the
\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}
precondition chain: histories no longer enforce the fine-grained “no repeated delete / size bookkeeping” pattern, and a typical relaxation widens the precondition to
{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt insert}}~|~{\mathit{elem}=x}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}
.

Filesystem

Consider a path-tree API with create/delete/exists operations. Here deleting a non-empty directory reportedly succeeds, yet a child path still appears to exist—a path-metadata inconsistency our property surfaces. Thus, the global property is given by the following:

\displaystyle{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=\mathit{parent}(\mathit{chp})}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delRsp}}~|~{\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}

\displaystyle{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt extReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=\mathit{chp}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt extRsp}}~|~{\mathit{exists}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}

The
u
Hat
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt initReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initReq}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initReq}}~|~{{\tt true}}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}~:~{}

\displaystyle p{:}\mathsf{Path}{\,\dashrightarrow\,}c{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{\mathsf{Byte}}\,|\,{\mathit{isRoot}(\mathit{parent}(p))}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=\mathit{parent}(p)\land\mathit{isDir}(\mathit{content})}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}\;{p\;c}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~|~{\neg\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,

\displaystyle p{:}\mathsf{Path}{\,\dashrightarrow\,}c{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{\mathsf{Byte}}\,|\,{\neg\,\mathit{isRoot}(p)}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt initReq}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}\;{p}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=p}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~|~{\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}\;{p}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}\;{p\;c}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~|~{\neg\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,

\displaystyle p{:}\mathsf{Path}{\,\dashrightarrow\,}c{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{\mathsf{Byte}}\,|\,{\neg\,\mathit{isRoot}(\mathit{parent}(p))}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=\mathit{parent}(p)\land\mathit{isDir}(\mathit{content})}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~|~{\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{\bigl((\bullet\setminus{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}~|~{\mathit{path}=\mathit{parent}(p)}}{\rangle}})\bigr)^{\ast}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,{\tt unit}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{[}}}{}{{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtReq}}\;{p\;c}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~|~{\mathit{succ}}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}{\cdot}{\langle}{\boldsymbol{{\tt delReq}}\;{p}}{\rangle}{\cdot}{\bullet^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{]}}}{}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\sqcap}}\,

\displaystyle p{:}\mathsf{Path}{\,\dashrightarrow\,}c{:}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@lbrace}}}{}{{\nu}{:}{\mathsf{Byte}}\,|\,{\mathit{isRoot}(\mathit{parent}(p))}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.4883,0.0313}\mbox{\rm{\lx@text@rbrace}}^{u}}{}{\,\shortrightarrow\,}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\tt crtRsp}}~:~{}
